But I’m here because for over a year I’ve been trying to get someone to take seriously that the new ticket rotation and the security audits are being used to build a paper trail against me at work.\n\nManagement keeps showing me logs and saying it’s just policy, like that explains everything. But in computer support, logs don’t tell the whole story. I’ve seen situations where something looks routine on paper, and then later it gets used to say, “See, this person is the problem.” It’s like when my old supervisor years ago used to say documentation is only as neutral as the person writing it—well, not exactly those words, but that was the idea.\n\nSo, I mean, could it theoretically not be about me? Maybe. But after a year of it lining up the way it has, it doesn’t feel random. It feels directed.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're being watched or monitored outside of your workplace?\n\nPatient: Outside of work? I don’t really know. I mean, I haven’t noticed, like, someone following me around or anything like that. Mostly what I pay attention to is the workplace stuff, because that’s where the logs and tickets and all that are.\n\nAlthough, you know, these days everything is connected anyway — phones, email, badge systems, all these accounts — but I don’t want to make it sound like I’m saying my family or neighbors are watching me. That’s not really what I’ve been trying to explain. My concern is more with management and what they’re documenting at work.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the belief that management is building a paper trail against you is something that might be happening only in your mind?\n\nPatient: I mean… I know why you’re asking that, but no, I don’t think it’s just in my mind. That’s the frustrating part. I’m here because for over a year now I’ve been trying to get someone to take seriously that the new ticket rotation and the security audits are being used to build a paper trail against me at work.\n\nManagement keeps showing me logs and saying, “It’s policy,” like that settles it. But I’ve worked in computer support a long time, and policies can be applied in a way that looks neutral but isn’t. I remember at another job, not the same thing exactly, but there were these reports people acted like were objective, and later they became ammunition. So when I see the pattern now, I’m not inventing it. I’m noticing what’s there.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a moment where you doubted your belief that management is intentionally building a case against you?\n\nPatient: I’ve had moments where I tried to doubt it, if that makes sense. Like I’ll sit there and think, okay, maybe the ticket rotation really is just some new system they rolled out, maybe the security audits are just policy like they keep saying. They show me logs and reports and act like that should end the discussion.\n\nBut then I look at how it’s been going on for over a year, and how the same things keep getting documented around me, and it’s hard to believe it’s just routine. I’m in computer support, so I know how these records can look neutral while still being used to build a case. So the doubt doesn’t really last long. It keeps coming back to feeling like they’re making a paper trail against me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's any possibility that your interpretation of the situation at work might be incorrect?\n\nPatient: I suppose anything is “possible,” sure. I mean, I worked in support long enough to know systems can be misunderstood—logs, permissions, ticket queues, all of that can look one way if you don’t have the full context.\n\nBut in this case… no, I don’t really think I’m reading it wrong. That’s why I’m here. For over a year I’ve been trying to get someone to understand that the new ticket rotation and these security audits aren’t just harmless policy. Management keeps showing me logs and saying, “See, it’s just procedure,” but that doesn’t prove it isn’t being used to build a paper trail.\n\nAnd I’ve seen this kind of thing before—not exactly the same, but the way documentation starts piling up, and later everyone acts like it was obvious all along. So yes, technically I could be wrong, but that’s not what it looks like to me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like someone or something was out to get you before this situation at work started?\n\nPatient: Before this specific thing at work? I mean… I wouldn’t say “out to get me” in some dramatic way. People use that phrase and it makes you sound paranoid, and that’s not what I’m trying to say.\n\nBut I’ve worked in computer support a long time, and I’ve seen how management can use procedures—ticket systems, audit logs, performance notes—to make something look objective. That’s what bothers me now. I’m here because for over a year I’ve been trying to get someone to take seriously that the new ticket rotation and the security audits are being used to build a paper trail against me, even though they keep showing me logs and saying it’s just policy.\n\nSo before this, no, not like this exactly. But I know how workplaces can set things up so it looks neutral when it really isn’t.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever believed someone was out to get you in a similar way before this situation at work?\n\nPatient: Similar before? Not really in the same way. I mean, I’ve had supervisors I didn’t trust, sure, but everybody has that at work. You learn after years in computer support that “policy” can mean a lot of things depending on who’s holding the paperwork.\n\nBut this situation is different. I’m here because for over a year now I’ve been trying to get someone to take seriously that the new ticket rotation and the security audits are being used to build a paper trail against me. This is specific to what’s happening at work now.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had another job where you felt management was out to get you?\n\nPatient: Another job? Not exactly… I mean, I’ve had managers who liked to hide behind procedures, sure. In computer support, everything gets documented—tickets, timestamps, escalations—and people act like that makes it neutral. But if you’ve been around long enough, you know documentation can be shaped.\n\nThere was one place years ago where they changed the metrics after a reorganization, and everyone suddenly had to justify why calls took longer, even though the systems were ancient—sorry, that’s not really what you asked.\n\nWhat I’m trying to say is, no, I don’t think I had another job where I felt it this clearly. Not when the pattern is still there.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your belief that management is building a case against you could be a delusion?\n\nPatient: A delusion? I mean… that’s a strong word.
